CoPre-MoE: Consolidating and Preserving MoE for Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning
CoPre-MoE：面向多模态持续指令调优的MoE整合与保持
Tao Hu, Ling-Xiang Zhang, Da-Wei Zhou
cs.CV · cs.AI · cs.LG
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Multimodal continual instruction tuning requires multimodal large language models to sequentially acquire new capabilities without catastrophic forgetting. Mixture-of-experts (MoE) architectures offer a promising way to mitigate task interference through expert specialization and combination. However, while existing MoE-based methods avoid overwriting shared parameters by allocating separate experts for each task, this progressive expansion incurs severe cross-task redundancy and linear parameter growth. Specifically, experts across tasks often learn overlapping functions alongside task-specific capabilities. For instance, ScienceQA and TextVQA both locate relevant image regions. To curb parameter redundancy without inducing forgetting, we propose Consolidating and Preserving MoE (CoPre-MoE). For each incoming task, CoPre-MoE first learns task-specific MoE to capture specialized capabilities. To eliminate redundancy and reduce parameter growth across tasks, we design a shared-and-private expert architecture. Specifically, we first evaluate functional overlap to selectively consolidate overlapping experts across tasks into a fixed-capacity generic expert pool. We then factorize the remaining task-specific experts into shared projection bases and compact task-specific cores within a private expert pool. To mitigate forgetting, we construct the router by pairing each expert with learnable retrieval keys, where historical keys remain frozen during continual updates while newly added keys are regularized with an orthogonality penalty. At inference, input-dependent routing dynamically combines both pools without test-time task labels. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CoPre-MoE achieves superior performance while substantially reducing parameter overhead.
Chinese Translation
多模态持续指令调优要求多模态大语言模型在不发生灾难性遗忘的情况下顺序获取新能力。混合专家（MoE）架构通过专家专门化与组合，为缓解任务干扰提供了一种有前景的途径。然而，尽管现有的基于MoE的方法通过为每个任务分配独立专家来避免覆盖共享参数，这种渐进式扩展会带来严重的跨任务冗余和参数线性增长。具体而言，跨任务的专家常常在任务特定能力之外学习到重叠的功能。例如，ScienceQA和TextVQA都会定位相关图像区域。为了在不引发遗忘的情况下抑制参数冗余，我们提出整合与保持MoE（Consolidating and Preserving MoE，CoPre-MoE）。对于每个新到来的任务，CoPre-MoE首先学习任务特定的MoE以捕获专门化能力。为了消除冗余并减少跨任务的参数增长，我们设计了一种共享与私有专家架构。具体而言，我们首先评估功能重叠，以选择性地将跨任务的重叠专家整合到一个固定容量的通用专家池中。然后，我们将剩余的任务特定专家分解为共享投影基和私有专家池中的紧凑任务特定核心。为了缓解遗忘，我们通过将每个专家与可学习检索键配对来构建路由器，其中历史键在持续更新期间保持冻结，而新添加的键则使用正交性惩罚进行正则化。在推理时，依赖于输入的路由在没有测试时任务标签的情况下动态组合两个池。大量实验表明，CoPre-MoE在显著降低参数开销的同时实现了优越的性能。

Superfluid: Local AI Server for Distributed LLM Inference
Superfluid：面向分布式 LLM 推理的本地 AI 服务器
Prabod Rathnayaka, Isura Manchanayake, Srikandabala Kogul, Steve Siu, Fabian Waschkowski, Lukas Wesemann
cs.DC · cs.AI · cs.CL
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Running large language models (LLMs) on local devices keeps data private, removes per-token cost, cuts network latency, and keeps working without a connection. Two shifts now strain how such models are served. On one device, several agents share a locally served model with an interactive user. Across devices, a developer or small team often owns several capable machines from different vendors, which sit idle most of the day because every local server serves one runtime on one machine. We present Superfluid, an open-source LLM server built for local inference at both scales. On one device, Superfluid drives llama.cpp, MLX, or BaseRT in isolated worker processes through one scheduler that moves interactive requests ahead of agent work, computes a prompt shared by many requests once, and writes every token to a durable log before it reaches the client. Across devices, one Superfluid server turns a fleet of machines into a single API endpoint. Machines with different hardware serve copies of the same model, receive work according to their measured speed, and can join or fail under load without losing a request. For Qwen3.8-27B on an Apple M5 Pro, a chat sent while eight agents are working receives its first token in 1.5 s, against 291-612 s on llama-server, Ollama, and mlx_lm.server. On six machines from three chip vendors serving 64 simulated users for one hour, Superfluid answers all 5,041 requests while machines join one at a time and one is killed and rejoins; throughput grows from 112 to 534 tok/s and the median first-token time falls from 99 to 12 s. On twelve Mac Studio M3 Ultras it sustains 10,656 tok/s across 38,563 requests from 384 agents without a failure. Superfluid is available under the Apache-2.0 licence at https://github.com/basecompute/superfluid.
Chinese Translation
在本地设备上运行大语言模型（LLM）可以保持数据私密、消除按 token 计费的成本、降低网络延迟，并在没有连接的情况下继续工作。如今有两种转变正在给此类模型的服务方式带来压力。在单台设备上，多个智能体与一个交互式用户共享一个本地服务的模型。跨设备来看，一名开发者或一个小团队通常拥有来自不同厂商的几台性能强大的机器，而这些机器一天中大部分时间处于空闲状态，因为每个本地服务器都只在一台机器上服务一个运行时。我们提出 Superfluid，一个为单设备和跨设备两种规模下的本地推理而构建的开源 LLM 服务器。在单台设备上，Superfluid 通过一个调度器在隔离的工作进程中驱动 llama.cpp、MLX 或 BaseRT；该调度器将交互式请求排在智能体工作之前，对许多请求共享的提示只计算一次，并在每个 token 到达客户端之前将其写入持久化日志。跨设备时，一个 Superfluid 服务器将一组机器转化为单一 API 端点。具有不同硬件的机器服务同一模型的副本，根据其测得的速度接收工作，并且可以在负载下加入或发生故障而不丢失任何请求。对于在 Apple M5 Pro 上运行的 Qwen3.8-27B，在八个智能体正在工作时发送的一次聊天在 1.5 s 内收到其第一个 token，而在 llama-server、Ollama 和 mlx_lm.server 上则需要 291-612 s。在来自三家芯片厂商的六台机器上，为 64 个模拟用户服务一小时的情况下，Superfluid 在机器逐台加入、其中一台被杀死并重新加入的同时回答了全部 5,041 个请求；吞吐量从 112 增长到 534 tok/s，首个 token 时间的中位数从 99 s 下降到 12 s。在十二台 Mac Studio M3 Ultra 上，它在来自 384 个智能体的 38,563 个请求中持续保持 10,656 tok/s 且没有发生故障。Superfluid 依据 Apache-2.0 许可证提供，地址为 https://github.com/basecompute/superfluid。

Personalization of Basic and Moral Values Guiding LLM-Controlled Assistant Robots
指导LLM控制的助理机器人的基本价值观与道德价值观的个性化
Mateusz Wozniak, Qi Gao, Agnieszka Wykowska
cs.HC · cs.AI
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Personal assistant robots powered by large language models (LLMs) are likely to enter everyday life within the next decade, but the values that should guide their behaviour remain largely unspecified. Current alignment practice steers a single model toward an aggregate of human values, ignoring that individual users may prefer a robot whose value profile differs from their own. We administer parallel self- and robot-targeted versions of three established psycho-metric instruments - the simplified Schwartz Values Survey (SVS), the Portrait Values Questionnaire (PVQ-21), and the Moral Foundations Questionnaire (MFQ) - to potential future users (N=22), capturing both their personal values and the values they would want an assistant robot to embody. We compare the three instruments as personalization tools using the UEQ-S and benchmark fifteen contemporary LLMs against the resulting profiles. Participants wanted their robot to score markedly lower than themselves on personal-focus values and higher on social-focus values; moral profiles were nearly identical. LLMs prompted to act as robots shifted only weakly toward the desired profile, indicating that default model behaviour and prompt engineering are no substitute for explicit value personalization.

MeReader: An Offline, Privacy-Preserving, Progress-Aware AI Assistant for Narrative eBook Reading
MeReader：一种用于叙事性电子书阅读的离线、隐私保护、进度感知 AI 助手
Mohammed Efaz, Udo Bub, Itilekha Podder
cs.IR · cs.AI · cs.CL
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
This paper introduces MeReader, a privacy-preserving, offline and progress-aware AI assistant designed to function as a contextual companion for narrative reading on consumer hardware. Unlike conventional Large Language Model (LLM)-based systems that process documents/books online in an all-texts-at-once type of way, thereby risking narrative spoilers, MeReader confines both retrieval and generation to the reader's distinct position in the text. The system integrates a hybrid retrieval architecture, combining vector similarity, BM25, and summary embeddings, to ensure high fidelity to the reader's current context. We evaluate the system across classical English books using both quantitative comprehension metrics and qualitative LLM-based independent judging. The results identify a quality-latency frontier, showing that mid-sized local models (2b-4b) can achieve comprehension parity with larger counterparts (5b+) while maintaining acceptable response quality. Importantly, the proposed progress-aware boundary mechanism is shown to prevent future content leakage without compromising retrieval quality and correctness. These findings suggest that a localized, privacy-centric approach can augment, rather than replace, the digital reading experience, offering a good alternative to cloud-dependent services. Therein, there remains a lack of amalgamate systems which combine progress-aware, privacy-preserving, and narrative eBook reading with AI support. It is in this very gap that the development of MeReader came to be; synthesizing said strands into an offline, book-constrained assistant focused on enhancing comprehension without compromising narrative continuity or user privacy.

cs.LG / 42 / 2611.17462
Diffusion Tree Sampling for Inference Time Adaptation of Crystal Generative Models
用于晶体生成模型推理时自适应的扩散树采样
Daniel T. Levy, Vineet Jain, Tara Akhound-Sadegh, Sékou-Oumar Kaba, Siamak Ravanbakhsh
cs.LG · cond-mat.mtrl-sci · cs.AI
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
Diffusion-based generative models have become strong priors over plausible crystal structures, but turning that prior into promising candidates requires steering toward several conflicting properties at once. Fine-tuning is expensive and produces a new model for every new objective, while differentiable guidance is not applicable to many real-world tasks. We instead adapt these generative models at inference time using Diffusion Tree Sampling (DTS), a Monte Carlo tree search procedure over the denoising trajectory. We make two technical contributions on top of DTS that are particularly relevant for AI-for-science applications. First, we show how to use existing property-conditional generative models (e.g., models fine-tuned for classifier-free guidance) as guided proposals inside DTS, without distorting the target distribution. Second, we extend DTS to multi-objective sampling, so that a single search produces samples spanning an entire Pareto front. We instantiate both adaptations on de novo crystal generation: improving the stability of crystals generated by different models, using pretrained conditional models to generate crystals belonging to rare space groups or with high bulk modulus, and generating crystals along a Pareto front of high dielectric constant and high bandgap materials. Across these tasks, DTS-based inference-time adaptation successfully improves model outputs without modifying the models themselves.
Chinese Translation
基于扩散的生成模型已成为对合理晶体结构的强先验，但要将该先验转化为有前景的候选结构，需要同时朝向多个相互冲突的性质进行引导。微调成本高昂，并且每出现一个新目标都会产生一个新模型，而可微引导不适用于许多现实世界任务。我们转而使用扩散树采样（Diffusion Tree Sampling，DTS）在推理时自适应这些生成模型，DTS 是一种在去噪轨迹上进行的蒙特卡洛树搜索过程。我们在 DTS 之上做出两项技术贡献，这两项贡献与 AI-for-science 应用尤为相关。首先，我们展示如何将现有的性质条件生成模型（例如，为无分类器引导而微调的模型）用作 DTS 内部的引导提议，同时不扭曲目标分布。其次，我们将 DTS 扩展到多目标采样，使得单次搜索能够产生跨越整个帕累托前沿的样本。我们在从头晶体生成上实例化这两种自适应：提高由不同模型生成的晶体的稳定性，使用预训练条件模型生成属于稀有空间群或具有高体积模量的晶体，以及沿高介电常数和高带隙材料的帕累托前沿生成晶体。在这些任务中，基于 DTS 的推理时自适应成功改进了模型输出，而无需修改模型本身。

cs.LG / 43 / 2611.17650
Classifier-Guided Latent Diffusion for Small-Molecule Generation
用于小分子生成的分类器引导潜在扩散
Ali Forooghi, Luis Rueda, Alioune Ngom
cs.LG
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
Diffusion models have emerged as a promising class of generative models for molecular design because of their ability to learn complex chemical distributions and support conditional or guided generation. In this work, we investigate a latent diffusion pipeline for property-guided small-molecule generation. The proposed framework represents canonicalized SMILES strings using a frozen ChemBERTa encoder, trains a Transformer-based decoder to reconstruct SELFIES strings from molecular latent embeddings, and learns an unconditional denoising diffusion model in the ChemBERTa latent space. To enable property-directed generation, a differentiable latent property predictor is used to guide the diffusion sampling process. Generated SELFIES strings are converted to SMILES, validated using RDKit, and evaluated using molecular validity, uniqueness, novelty, and predicted property scores. We study guidance toward two drug-relevant binary endpoints: blood--brain barrier permeability and inhibition of human $β$-secretase 1. The results suggest that latent diffusion over pretrained molecular embeddings can generate chemically valid and novel molecules while enabling controllable steering toward desired molecular properties.

cs.LG / 44 / 2611.17735
Logically Consistent Accurate Forecasts
逻辑一致且准确的预测
Kate Yuan, Rohan Alur, Manish Raghavan, Devavrat Shah
cs.LG · cs.AI
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Agentic forecasting systems based on large language models (LLMs) are now competitive with expert human forecasters, but these systems still suffer from a critical limitation: the inability to produce forecasts that are logically consistent. For example, forecasts for related events often fail to satisfy the axioms of probability, which in turn implies that related forecasts cannot simultaneously be correct and undermines their utility for decision makers. Enforcing consistency is not trivial -- naive approaches (e.g., projection onto the set of consistent forecasts) can degrade accuracy. We argue that the key to overcoming the apparent tradeoff between consistency and accuracy lies in the reasoning which supports each forecast. We finetune a supervisory language model that leverages this reasoning to jointly reconcile inconsistencies while improving the accuracy of related forecasts. On ForecastBench, our method improves the Brier score of a retrieval-augmented GPT-5.1 forecaster by 9.3% while reducing measures of structural inconsistency by 82-90%. We interrogate our method via a comprehensive series of baselines and ablations, and find that no alternative method matches our gains in consistency and accuracy simultaneously.

cs.LG / 45 / 2611.17746
Complex Schrödinger Bridges
复薛定谔桥
Dong-Sig Han, Tolga Birdal
cs.LG · stat.ML
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
Diffusion bridges have emerged as versatile frameworks for facilitating transformations between arbitrary data distributions. However, these approaches are hindered by an immense optimization space and its underlying high-order structures. This paper explores a variant of diffusion bridges for solving real-world problems in complex vector spaces governed by scaling and rotation laws, such as Hamiltonian structures. To address this, we introduce conformal optimal transport (COT), a complex-valued transportation framework designed for physical systems with geometric and dissipative dynamics under a geometric lifting. Building on this, we propose a new class of dynamical diffusion bridges, called complex Schrödinger bridges (CSBs), whose derived algorithms can handle diffusion-based tasks using a conformal geometric prior. For scalability, we propose image-to-image CSB (I$^2$CSB), a tractable diffusion-bridge technique with an analytic spiral posterior. Finally, we implement a physics-informed CSB algorithm within simulations of quantum electrodynamics, mimicking the logical behavior of quantum logic gates. Our extensive results validate the underlying theory while highlighting advantages of complex-valued diffusion.

cs.LG / 46 / 2611.17772
Antidistillation is Cheap: Lightweight Distillation-Resistance via Stylistic Corruption
反蒸馏成本低廉：通过风格破坏实现轻量级抗蒸馏
Anh Tu Ngo, Anupam Chattopadhyay
cs.LG
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Knowledge distillation (KD) is a highly effective paradigm for transferring capabilities from large language models (LLMs) to smaller, more specialized student models. In commercial deployment where frontier models are restricted behind black-box APIs, sequence-level distillation remains a potent vector for competitors to clone proprietary capabilities at a fraction of the development cost. Existing defenses mostly rely on surrogate models to manipulate next-token sampling, which increases computational overhead and real-time inference latency. In this work, we propose a lightweight defense that dynamically injects style-steering vectors into the teacher model's activations upon malicious queries. This framework offers exceptional flexibility, allowing hosts to customize target defensive styles and dynamically blend them to maximize stylistic variance. Crucially, this inference-time intervention preserves semantic utility and fluency during malicious queries, misleading adversaries into believing that they are extracting pristine teacher outputs, while standard consumers continue to receive original, unmodified responses. Our experiments demonstrate that this framework successfully disrupts student learning, achieving substantial anti-distillation performance while incurring negligible overhead and preserving text naturalness. Our code is available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/distill-resist-38C6/.
Chinese Translation
知识蒸馏（KD）是一种将从大语言模型（LLMs）向更小、更专门化的学生模型迁移能力的高效范式。在前沿模型被限制于黑盒 API 之后的商业部署中，序列级蒸馏仍然是竞争对手以一小部分开发成本克隆专有能力的有力途径。现有防御大多依赖代理模型来操纵下一 token 采样，这增加了计算开销和实时推理延迟。在本工作中，我们提出一种轻量级防御，在恶意查询时动态地将风格引导向量注入教师模型的激活中。该框架提供了卓越的灵活性，允许宿主定制目标防御风格并动态混合它们，以最大化风格方差。关键的是，这种推理时干预在恶意查询期间保持了语义效用和流畅性，误导对手相信他们正在提取原始的教师输出，而标准消费者继续收到原始、未修改的响应。我们的实验表明，该框架成功干扰了学生学习，在实现显著抗蒸馏性能的同时仅产生可忽略的开销并保持文本自然性。我们的代码可在 https://anonymous.4open.science/r/distill-resist-38C6/ 获取。

cs.LG / 47 / 2611.17851
Electrocardiographic Imaging via Posterior Sampling under Operator Discrepancy
算子差异下基于后验采样的心电成像
Ricarda Buttmann, Andreas M. Lehrmann
cs.LG
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
Electrocardiographic imaging (ECGI) reconstructs heart-surface potentials from body-surface potentials, an ill-posed inverse problem. Diffusion posterior samplers combine a learned prior with an explicit forward operator that they assume to be accurate. In ECGI this operator is only an estimate, because the body changes with respiration and posture, and body composition such as subcutaneous fat is rarely known for an individual patient. The resulting operator discrepancy is structured and depends on the heart signal. We introduce OpDis-PS, a diffusion posterior sampler that models this discrepancy instead of treating it as signal-independent noise. OpDis-PS learns how the discrepancy depends on the signal, folds this into a corrected forward operator, and reconstructs with a fixed prior across all operator conditions. We study two realistic sources of discrepancy, changing lung conductivity and an added subcutaneous-fat layer. Across both, OpDis-PS reconstructs more accurately than classical regularization and learned predictors, with its largest gains on held-out cardiac pathologies.

cs.LG / 48 / 2611.17857
Fuse-All MoE Inference Kernel with Load Balancing on TPUs
在 TPU 上具有负载均衡的 Fuse-All MoE 推理内核
Rupeng Liu, Bangsheng Tang, Zongwei Zhou, Chengji Yao, Yejin Lee
cs.LG
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures activate only a subset of experts for each token, allowing large language models (LLMs) to scale parameter capacity without a proportional increase in computation per token. However, intermediate data movement and uneven expert loads limit the efficiency of MoE inference on TPUs. To address these bottlenecks, we introduce Fuse-All MoE, a TPU kernel design that jointly optimizes expert computation, output communication, and load balancing. The kernel fuses token collection, expert projections, gated activation, and output writes, retaining expert computation intermediates on chip. Fixed token-slot destinations allow scheduling and expert placement to change while preserving routing semantics. In prefill-decode (PD) disaggregated serving, Fuse-All MoE balances EP16 prefill workloads through scheduling and expert redistribution, while symmetric EP8TP2 shares active expert computation between the two TensorCore devices within each chip during decode. Our experiments show that Fuse-All MoE reduces kernel runtime for a measured skewed EP16 prefill workload from 2.3x to 1.09x the same fixed reference time for a balanced workload. Across 32-512 decode tokens, symmetric EP8TP2 reduces kernel latency by up to 21.3% relative to fused EP16. With accompanying serving stack optimizations, the integrated system improves serving throughput by up to 34.5% over the Megablox EP16 baseline.

混合专家（MoE）架构对每个 token 仅激活一部分专家，使大语言模型（LLM）能够扩展参数容量，而不会带来每个 token 计算量的成比例增长。然而，中间数据的搬运以及专家负载的不均衡限制了 MoE 在 TPU 上的推理效率。为解决这些瓶颈，我们提出 Fuse-All MoE，一种联合优化专家计算、输出通信与负载均衡的 TPU 内核设计。该内核将 token 收集、专家投影、门控激活与输出写回融合在一起，把专家计算的中间结果保留在片上。固定的 token 槽位目的地使调度与专家放置可以改变，同时保持路由语义不变。在预填充-解码（PD）分离式服务中，Fuse-All MoE 通过调度与专家重分配来均衡 EP16 的预填充负载，而在解码阶段，对称的 EP8TP2 在每个芯片内的两个 TensorCore 设备之间共享活跃专家计算。我们的实验表明，对于一个实测的偏斜 EP16 预填充负载，Fuse-All MoE 将其内核运行时间从均衡负载下同一固定参考时间的 2.3 倍降低到 1.09 倍。在 32-512 个解码 token 范围内，对称 EP8TP2 相对融合 EP16 将内核延迟最多降低 21.3%。配合相应的服务栈优化，该集成系统相对 Megablox EP16 基线将服务吞吐量最多提升 34.5%。

Disentangling Forgetting from Utility: Lightweight LLM Unlearning via Orthogonal Null-Space Projection
解耦遗忘与效用：基于正交零空间投影的轻量级LLM遗忘
Xiuyuan Wang, Weiming Liu, Zongxin Yang, Fan Wang, Liemiao Jin, Rubin Qin, Wei Chen, Bin Shi, Chaochao Chen, Xiaolin Zheng
cs.LG
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) pose significant security risks by retaining private or hazardous information, creating an urgent need for unlearning techniques that excise specific knowledge without compromising general utility. However, prevailing fine-tuning-based unlearning methods struggle to achieve this balance, often suffering from computational inefficiency and unintended utility degradation due to the entangled nature of knowledge within model parameters. To disentangle forgetting from utility, we propose UUE, a theoretically grounded framework that reformulates unlearning as controllable model editing. First, UUE projects parameter updates into the orthogonal left null space of retained knowledge representations, strictly ensuring that the editing process causes minimal interference to the model's general capabilities. Second, distinct from targeted mapping, we introduce a convex untargeted objective that derives a closed-form analytical solution to effectively disrupt undesired knowledge by pushing forget-set embeddings toward their antipodal points. We implement UUE via lightweight, pluggable adapters (including a low-rank variant, UUE-L), enabling efficient deployment without modifying the frozen backbone. Extensive experiments on TOFU and WMDP benchmarks demonstrate that UUE and UUE-L achieve superior unlearning efficacy, significantly outperforming existing methods.

TIDE: Reclaiming Lost Performance In On-Policy Reinforcement Learning
TIDE：在策略强化学习中重获失去的性能
Mathew Jacob, Morgan Borjigin-Wang, Kan Zhu, Po Hao Chen, Yi Pan, Andy Ruan, Nayani Modugula, Ethan Lavi, Luis Ceze, Malte Schwarzkopf, Akshay Narayan, Deepti Raghavan, Stephanie Wang
cs.LG · cs.DC · cs.PF
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Synchronous, colocated RL post-training of large language models (LLMs) today suffers from severe under-utilization due to long-tail rollouts. Inference engines fall idle waiting for long-tail rollouts or decode at low, inefficient batch sizes. To address this, we present TIDE, an RL training system that preserves on-policy training semantics while recovering resource utilization. TIDE does so by two complementary techniques: (1) dynamically repurposing rollout GPUs for training while long-tail rollouts complete and (2) dynamically migrating rollouts between inference engines to boost decoding throughput. TIDE automatically determines the right time to repurpose rollout resources through an adaptive migration policy, obtaining performance improvements that generalize across multiple applications and hardware configurations without requiring bespoke configuration. In our evaluation, TIDE provides end-to-end speedups of up to 1.36x over colocated systems, outperforming all evaluated on-policy baselines and achieving up to 97% of optimal throughput.

Analyzing Learning-Dynamics Across Loss and Prediction Choices for Diffusion Models
分析扩散模型在不同损失与预测选择下的学习动态
Dongwoo Kim
cs.LG
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
Denoising generative models predict clean data ($x$), noise ($ε$), or velocity ($v$), while training losses can be measured in any of these spaces. Although the resulting nine combinations are algebraically related, they can yield substantially different denoising accuracy and sample quality. We disentangle the effects of prediction and loss spaces. For a fixed prediction target, we show that changing the loss space is exactly equivalent to timestep reweighting and provide a heuristic analysis of how this reweighting affects generation quality. We then show that a rank-$m$ linear bottleneck compressing a $D$-dimensional noisy input imposes an irreducible squared-error floor of $D-m$ on noise and velocity prediction, whereas clean-data prediction can exploit low-dimensional data structure. Combining this bound with prediction-dependent error conversion yields distinct necessary conditions on bottleneck capacity. Experiments with U-Net and JiT across CIFAR-10, Imagenette, and ImageNet-256 support these theoretical predictions. Under compression, prediction choice strongly affects both denoising accuracy and sample quality, with noise-prediction errors closely matching the theoretical lower bound. Our results provide a quantitative understanding of how architectural bottlenecks interact with prediction and loss choices in denoising generative models.

Deep Latents, Long Memory: Latent persistence explains temporal correlations in transformer activations
深层隐变量，长久记忆：隐变量持续性解释 Transformer 激活中的时间相关性
Francesco Cagnetta, Santiago Acevedo
cs.LG · cs.CL
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
The hidden layers of Large Language Models (LLMs) are believed to encode representations of the data whose abstraction increases with depth. However, in the absence of an operational notion of abstraction, this statement is hard to test quantitatively. Here, we propose an alternative view, building on the principle that the correlations of a sequence reflect the structure of the process that generated it. We define the autocorrelation function of a layer as the average dot product between its activations at two context positions separated by a lag. Latent variables encoded persistently along the context create slowly decaying tails in the autocorrelation, and we find these tails to grow across layers in both synthetic and natural data. In synthetic data with controlled hierarchical latent structure, this growth stems from the encoding of abstract latent variables, which govern long stretches of the input. Indeed, the persistence of latent codes predicts the qualitative structure of autocorrelations and accounts for most of the empirical measures. In real text, we take sparse-autoencoder features as proxies for latent variables, and find that their persistence correlates strongly with the magnitude of autocorrelations across datasets and LLMs. In particular, the persistence time increases with depth, as do autocorrelations, implying that deeper latent features persist for longer context spans. We complement this analysis by explicitly ablating the persistent sparse-autoencoder features and observing a consequent decrease in the autocorrelations.

STAMP: Manufacturing-Informed Generative Design and Shape Optimization of Sheet Panels
STAMP：制造信息驱动的薄板面板生成式设计与形状优化
Jihoon Kim, Jun Hyeok Song, Wonho Ha, Namwoo Kang
cs.LG
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
Generative design of formed sheet panels must preserve geometric manufacturing conditions while exploring layouts and improving performance. STAMP embeds these conditions directly in the shape representation. Each panel is described by its material layout and a reference surface with one height at each occupied position in a plane perpendicular to the drawing direction. Nominal thickness is prescribed along the normal. The representation allows curved surfaces, ribs, and openings. It excludes reference-surface undercuts and keeps nominal thickness fixed without manufacturing penalty terms. Because these conditions belong to the representation, every generated and optimized panel satisfies them regardless of training-set size. Boundary-conditioned diffusion generates alternative layouts, and predictor gradients guide a surface optimization that retains outlines, openings, and designated fixed regions. We demonstrate STAMP on automotive hood inner panels using CarHoods10k. With a comparable number of stored values, depth maps reconstruct surfaces more accurately than point-cloud and voxel baselines. From only five training designs, one of three training runs yields novel and varied layouts. A generator trained on 500 designs, 6.5% of the training set, already reaches the layout variety of the full-data model. Across 11 held-out design families, predictor-based selection among existing dataset designs yields an average gap of 0.18 mm to the best available FEA deformation, versus an expected 1.59 mm for random selection. It does better than random selection in all 11 families. At a 10% depth-change bound, a network not used in the search predicts an average deformation reduction of 24.8% for six generated and existing panels, with a 3.2% average mass increase computed from geometry. Engineers can thus explore and optimize panels within this manufacturing-informed design space.

NeoResearch: Literature- and Experience-Guided Autonomous Time Series Model Discovery
NeoResearch：文献与经验引导的自主时间序列模型发现
Ze Guo, Mingyue Cheng, Xiaoyu Tao, Shuo Yu, Shangtong Ye
cs.LG
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Developing effective forecasting models for diverse time-series domains requires substantial expert knowledge, evidence from scientific literature on the web, and iterative experimentation. However, translating this knowledge and research experience into model designs requires preserving source context and testing applicability under task-specific assumptions. In this paper, we propose NeoResearch, a literature- and experience-guided autonomous research framework that leverages large language models (LLMs) as researchers to discover lightweight, domain-specific forecasting models. Specifically, NeoResearch establishes an expert-inspired dual-loop research workflow, coupling hypothesis-driven architecture exploration with iterative training and hyperparameter optimization through experimental feedback. To enable knowledge-grounded discovery, the agent autonomously mines scientific literature from the Web, extracting modeling mechanisms, empirical evidence, and applicability conditions to formulate task-grounded, testable architecture hypotheses. Furthermore, a hierarchical experience memory links literature findings to experimental outcomes and consolidates conditional lessons, guiding architecture discovery and training optimization while supporting reuse across forecasting tasks. Experiments on ten datasets show competitive performance and support dual loops, literature extraction, and memory reuse. Our code is available.

cs.LG / 55 / 2611.18274
Right Token, Right Time: From Per-Token Gating to Trajectory-Aware Activation Steering
正确的 token，正确的时机：从逐 token 门控到轨迹感知的激活引导
Nima Eshraghi, Lovedeep Gondara, Sagarika Suresh, Xiaotong Xu, Jithin Pradeep, Fanny Chevalier
cs.LG · cs.AI
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Activation steering via sparse autoencoders (SAEs) enables behavioral control of large language models without task-specific fine-tuning. Yet, standard methods apply the steering signal at every generated token, displacing representations from the distribution the model was trained on and eroding generation quality. We show that \emph{when} along a generation the intervention is applied is a decisive axis of control. We cast steering as a per-token allocation problem under a fixed budget of steerable tokens, so that methods differ only in which tokens they select, making allocation the sole variable. Within this framework we introduce two gates. The first, Contrastive Feature Projection (CFP), is memoryless and selects the tokens least aligned with the target behavior through a sparse, interpretable feature projection. The second, Closed-Loop Adaptive Bernoulli Steering (CLABS), is trajectory aware. It treats allocation as closed-loop feedback control, tracking the alignment a generation has accumulated so far and spending budget where the trajectory falls behind. On a linear steering-response model we derive an explicit gain region that guarantees closed-loop stability of CLABS and show that its integral term reduces the steady-state tracking offset. Empirically, CFP and CLABS achieve the largest behavioral shift among budgeted gates at matched budgets, with CLABS strongest under the tight budgets where allocation matters most, while best preserving fluency.

通过稀疏自编码器（SAEs）进行的激活引导能够在不进行任务特定微调的情况下实现大语言模型的行为控制。然而，标准方法在每个生成的 token 上都施加引导信号，使表示偏离模型训练时所处的分布，并损害生成质量。我们表明，在一次生成过程中何时施加干预是一个决定性的控制维度。我们将引导建模为在可引导 token 的固定预算下的逐 token 分配问题，使得不同方法仅在选择哪些 token 上有所不同，从而使分配成为唯一的变量。在该框架内，我们引入两种门控。第一种是对比特征投影（Contrastive Feature Projection, CFP），它是无记忆的，并通过稀疏、可解释的特征投影选择与目标行为最不一致的 token。第二种是闭环自适应伯努利引导（Closed-Loop Adaptive Bernoulli Steering, CLABS），它是轨迹感知的。它将分配视为闭环反馈控制，跟踪一次生成截至目前已累积的对齐程度，并将预算花费在轨迹落后的地方。在一个线性引导响应模型上，我们推导出一个显式的增益区域，该区域保证 CLABS 的闭环稳定性，并表明其积分项减小了稳态跟踪偏移。在经验上，在匹配预算下，CFP 和 CLABS 在预算受限的门控中实现了最大的行为偏移，其中 CLABS 在分配最为重要的紧预算下最强，同时最好地保持了流畅性。

f-Divergence Self-Play for Tabular Anomaly Detection via Large Language Models
f-散度自博弈：通过大语言模型实现表格异常检测
Hoang Tran Vuong, Linh Ngo Van, Dang Nguyen, Thin Nguyen, Phuoc Nguyen, Mehrtash Harandi, Trung Le
cs.LG · cs.CL
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Anomaly detection in tabular data poses significant challenges due to heterogeneous feature types-mixing numerical, categorical, and textual attributes, which complicate learning meaningful representations of normality. Recent work has applied large language models (LLMs) to this problem by serializing table rows as text sequences, yet these approaches rely on one-shot supervised fine-tuning that offers limited signal to tighten the model's description of normality. We propose DiSPaT, a self-play fine-tuning framework that strengthens the model's understanding of normal data. Building on the theoretical foundation of f-divergence minimization, we derive a tight approximation connecting our training objective to reducing the distributional gap between real normal data and model-generated samples. DiSPaT operates through an alternating optimization: at each iteration, the current policy generates synthetic samples that serve as pseudo-anomalies, while a critic discriminator learns to distinguish these from real normal samples; this signal drives policy updates that progressively align the model distribution with the true normal-data distribution. Extensive experiments on diverse benchmarks demonstrate that DiSPaT consistently outperforms prior LLM-based methods, deep learning approaches, and classical unsupervised detectors for tabular anomaly detection.

cs.LG / 57 / 2611.18453
Black-Box Uncertainty Quantification for Large Language Models via Ensemble-of-Ensembles
基于集成之集成的大语言模型黑盒不确定性量化
Wang Ma, Debarun Bhattacharjya, Junkyu Lee, Nhan H Pham, Harsha Kokel, Qiang Ji
cs.LG · cs.AI
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Reliable use of large language models (LLMs) requires uncertainty estimates that separate ambiguity intrinsic to a prompt from genuine knowledge gaps. Bayesian and deep-ensemble methods deliver this aleatoric/epistemic decomposition in principle, but are computationally prohibitive at LLM scale and require white-box access. Existing black-box methods, in contrast, summarize to a single scalar that cannot make the distinction. We introduce a two-level ensemble that bridges this gap. Stochastic decoding of a fixed prompt forms the inner ensemble; meaning-preserving semantic perturbations of the prompt form the outer ensemble. Variability is measured in a continuous embedding space, and the law of total covariance gives an exact within-/between-perturbation decomposition to estimate aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty (AU/EU); it requires only black-box sampling access. On top of this estimator, we contribute three theoretical results that ground the framework: (i) a finite-sample bias identity for nested Monte Carlo with a closed-form bias-corrected epistemic estimator; (ii) a Bayesian bridge bounding the gap between perturbation-population AU/EU and Bayesian targets; and (iii) a separation result identifying a class of failure modes that any consistency-only black-box method provably misses while perturbation-based EU detects. Across five short QA benchmarks and four instruction-tuned LLMs spanning two families and two scales, our estimator is competitive with strong white-box baselines, and at a matched generation budget it matches leading black-box methods on error ranking while decomposing AU and EU; controlled paired interventions show that AU and EU respond selectively to distinct, actionable sources of failure: prompt ambiguity and model knowledge gaps; and a long-form pilot on the NQ long-answer split shows the framework extends to claim-level factuality without retraining.

cs.LG / 58 / 2611.18553
WeightBench: Evaluating Language Models as Weight-Space Programmers
WeightBench：评估语言模型作为权重空间程序员
Aayush Marasini, Barsat Khadka, Nick Rahimi
cs.LG · cs.AI
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Recent work shows that large language models (LLMs) can operate directly on the parameters of other neural networks. They can read what a network was trained to do, fix a network that fails, and write working networks from scratch, all without gradient descent. This capability has been termed Weight-Space Programming (WSP). Exploring this new paradigm systematically requires a robust benchmark. We introduce WEIGHTBENCH, a benchmark that evaluates LLMs of increasing capability on 42 WSP tasks across five tracks: reading, fixing and writing weights, recognizing edits that are impossible, and data-free compression. The networks whose weights the LLMs read and edit range from 2K-parameter control policies to 7B-parameter language models. Experimental benchmark results show that frontier agents can already read small networks and write working control policies without ever running them, but cannot yet patch language models, compress them without data, or tell an impossible edit from a hard one. On all 42 tasks, Claude Opus 5.5 reaches a macro score of 0.535 (Sonnet 5.5 0.466, Haiku 5.5 0.402, DeepSeek v4-flash 0.215); on the 38 tasks it was run on, GPT-6.1-sol reaches 0.568 against Opus's 0.546. Code, the task registry, our OS-level sandbox harness, and the live leaderboard are publicly available at https://github.com/Aayush-Marasini/WeightBench.

When Should a Virtual Patient Ask? RISP-Guided Proactive Information Seeking in Multi-Agent Patient Simulation
虚拟患者应在何时提问？多智能体患者模拟中由RISP引导的主动信息寻求
Jingyao Cai, Shuaijun Liu, Abdul Rehman, Xiaowan Li, Qintian, Thomas Dolby, Sue M Green, Xiaosong Yang
cs.MA
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Virtual patient (VP) systems support nursing communication training by providing simulated encounters in which learners practise explaining care and responding to patient concerns. Although large language models (LLMs) support flexible dialogue, current approaches give less explicit attention to how patients' information needs develop and guide the content and timing of their questions. We draw on the Risk Information Seeking and Processing (RISP) model, which relates information seeking to perceived risk and the gap between perceived knowledge and desired understanding. Guided by this perspective, we propose the RISP Multi-Agent System (RISP-MAS), a VP system for patient-initiated information seeking. The system links ongoing assessment of the patient's perceptions and information needs to coordinated decisions about whether to seek information, when to ask and which uncertainty to address. Evaluation of nursing student-VP interactions suggested that RISP-MAS exhibited plausible patient information-seeking behaviour, as indicated by expert assessments, while student feedback reflected positive interaction experiences. This work extends patient simulation by connecting a psychological account of information needs to explicit decisions about question initiation. The approach offers nursing students opportunities to practise recognising unresolved patient concerns and tailoring explanations to patients' information needs.

Imitation Policies Learn a Proprioceptive Anchor: A Predictive Law for Perception Noise and Its Closed Loop Cost
模仿策略学习一个本体感觉锚点：感知噪声的预测定律及其闭环代价
Christopher Malili
cs.RO · cs.AI · cs.LG
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
Imitation policies for robot manipulation are often trained on noisy object positions, and they learn to discount the noisy reading. We show how much they discount it, and why. Because demonstrators steer toward the object, the robot's own state carries information about where the object is. A trained policy combines this proprioceptive anchor with the reading and weights the reading by $α= V/(V + s^2)$, where $s^2$ is the reading noise and $V$ the anchor's error variance. $V$ belongs to the learner and is far smaller than a linear model of the demonstrations implies, which is why classical errors-in-variables (EIV) regression overestimates $α$. Measured once, $V$ predicted ten held-out sensitivities across two robomimic tasks, two learners and four new noise levels, with mean absolute error 0.025 against 0.37 for classical EIV, and a parameter-free bias test rules out the simpler explanation that the policies just overestimate the noise. The discount does not carry over to deployment, where the policy produces its own state: closed-loop sensitivity to a persistent reading offset exceeded $α$ in all 15 policies that passed the registered gates, although the stricter registered prediction, that all would follow the offset fully, held for 8 of them. Post hoc, lower $α$ went with lower success, steeply for diffusion, and a registered prospective test confirmed this for diffusion on Can at two new noise levels: the measured $α$ predicted success before any rollout, within 0.03 (95% intervals $\pm 0.08$), although training noise predicts it equally well. Every test was registered before its data, including the classical benchmark that failed, and all other analyses are labelled post hoc; all results are in simulation with synthetic Gaussian noise.

cs.SE / 61 / 2611.17581
Beyond Source Code: Evaluating Natural Language Specifications for LLM-Based C-to-Rust Translation
超越源代码：评估用于基于LLM的C到Rust翻译的自然语言规范
Biruk Tadesse, Lawrence Arkoh, Marcelo D'Amorim, Wesley K. G. Assunção
cs.SE
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) have shown promising results for C-to-Rust translation, yet existing approaches primarily rely on source code as input, leaving natural-language specifications of program behavior underexplored as an alternative or complementary representation. We hypothesize that direct code translation encourages LLMs to preserve C-specific implementation choices, producing Rust that may be functionally correct but unnecessarily verbose, reliant on unsafe constructs, and less idiomatic. We investigate this hypothesis by comparing three translation settings: code-only, specification-only, and code-and-specification. Using 300 C programs from the IBM CodeNet dataset, we evaluate GPT-5.2 and GPT-4o-mini on functional correctness, maintainability, complexity, idiomaticity, unsafe construct usage, and cost. Code-only translation achieves the highest test pass rate (98.6%) but often mirrors C's implementation patterns. In contrast, specification-only translation produces the most idiomatic and compact Rust, reducing unsafe blocks by approximately 6x and Clippy style warnings by 2.4x. However, using specifications without validation yields test pass rates as low as 86% with our best performing model. Adding an iterative test-based validation and refinement step for specifications raises this to a 95.3% test pass rate. Overall, specification-based translation consistently improves code quality relative to code-only translation, while code-and-specification offers the best balance between correctness (98.3% test pass rate) and maintainability. Our study systematically compares these translation settings, highlighting the trade-offs among specification quality, translation correctness, code quality, and computational cost.

大语言模型（LLM）在C到Rust翻译中已显示出有前景的结果，然而现有方法主要依赖源代码作为输入，将程序行为的自然语言规范作为替代或补充表示的研究仍不充分。我们假设，直接的代码翻译会促使LLM保留C特有的实现选择，从而生成功能上可能正确但过度冗长、依赖不安全构造且不够地道的Rust。我们通过比较三种翻译设置来研究这一假设：仅代码、仅规范、代码与规范。使用IBM CodeNet数据集中的300个C程序，我们在功能正确性、可维护性、复杂性、地道性、不安全构造使用和成本方面评估GPT-5.2和GPT-4o-mini。仅代码翻译达到最高测试通过率（98.6%），但常常镜像C的实现模式。相比之下，仅规范翻译生成最地道且最紧凑的Rust，将不安全块减少约6倍，将Clippy风格警告减少2.4倍。然而，在没有验证的情况下使用规范，即使使用我们表现最好的模型，测试通过率也低至86%。为规范添加基于测试的迭代验证与细化步骤，可将这一通过率提高到95.3%。总体而言，基于规范的翻译相对于仅代码翻译持续提高代码质量，而代码与规范则在正确性（98.3%测试通过率）和可维护性之间提供最佳平衡。我们的研究系统地比较了这些翻译设置，突出了规范质量、翻译正确性、代码质量和计算成本之间的权衡。

cs.LG / 62 / 2611.18467
Score-based neural ordinary differential equations for reaction-diffusions
基于得分的神经常微分方程用于反应-扩散
Jianguo Hou, Wuchen Li
math.NA · cs.LG
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
A diffusion equation can be rewritten as a transport equation driven by the score function of the evolving density. Conventional probability-flow formulations, which move particles deterministically with this score-driven velocity, typically conserve total mass. In this work, we develop a Lagrangian method for reaction-diffusion equations in which each particle carries a position and a physical weight. The characteristic ODEs incorporate source terms into the evolution of the log-density and its gradient, while particle weights track changes in mass. We combine this representation with a learned velocity field and weighted particle training to approximate these dynamics. For non-increasing reaction rates, a relative-entropy inequality supports the mass-normalized self-consistency residual as the training objective. This objective can control the error of the induced density when the growth rate is bounded. The algorithm divides the time interval into short windows. In each window, a frozen velocity generates the particle data for a regression of the network velocity. We draw the particles from period-adapted initial sampling distributions and weight them in the loss partly by their predicted future mass, because the reaction can make regions with little initial mass important later. We numerically investigate the method on the Ornstein--Uhlenbeck (OU) equation with a linear source term, Fisher--KPP equations, and Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao gradient flows.

Abstract
Commercial solvers are the standard tool for difficult scheduling problems. cs.LG / 64 / 2611.18124
Muon Attains $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-3})$ Iteration Complexity under Shuffling
Muon 在乱序采样下达到 $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-3})$ 的迭代复杂度
Kun Huang, Shi Pu, Karl Henrik Johansson
math.OC · cs.LG
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Muon is a stochastic first-order optimizer for matrix-valued parameters. It maintains an exponential moving average of stochastic gradients in a momentum buffer with coefficient $β$, and orthogonalizes the buffer to obtain the update direction. Muon has been reported to accelerate and stabilize the training of large language models. In practice, it is often used with shuffling (sampling without replacement), where each epoch visits the components of a finite-sum objective in a permuted order. However, its iteration complexity in this setting remains open. We analyze Muon under shuffling, with the momentum buffer orthogonalized by the polar map. For smooth nonconvex finite-sum problems, we establish an $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-3})$ iteration complexity for finding an $\varepsilon$-stationary point. This guarantee holds for every sequence of permutations, covering random reshuffling, shuffle once, and the incremental gradient method. This complexity matches that of stochastic gradient descent under shuffling in its dependence on $\varepsilon$, and improves on Muon's $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-4})$ complexity under sampling with replacement. Our guarantee requires a problem-dependent $β$ that approaches one as the number of components grows. In contrast, no fixed $β$ guarantees convergence to stationarity for every number of components. We show this by constructing a family of smooth convex problems on which, for any fixed $β\in[0,1)$, Muon remains bounded away from stationarity once the number of components is sufficiently large. This failure occurs for every sequence of permutations and persists even with small stepsizes. These results suggest two practical tuning rules: if the training loss stops decreasing even after reducing the stepsize, increase $β$; when increasing $β$, reduce the stepsize accordingly.
Chinese Translation
Muon 是一种用于矩阵值参数的随机一阶优化器。它在系数为 $β$ 的动量缓冲区中维护随机梯度的指数移动平均，并对该缓冲区进行正交化以获得更新方向。已有报告称 Muon 能够加速并稳定大语言模型的训练。在实践中，它常与乱序采样（shuffling，无放回采样）一起使用，其中每个 epoch 按某种排列顺序访问有限和目标（finite-sum objective）的各个分量。然而，其在该设定下的迭代复杂度仍是一个未解决的问题。我们分析了采用极映射（polar map）对动量缓冲区进行正交化的 Muon 在乱序采样下的表现。对于光滑非凸有限和问题，我们建立了寻找 $\varepsilon$-稳定点所需的 $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-3})$ 迭代复杂度。该保证对每一种排列序列都成立，涵盖随机重排（random reshuffling）、shuffle once 以及增量梯度方法。该复杂度在依赖于 $\varepsilon$ 的方式上与乱序采样下的随机梯度下降相匹配，并改进了 Muon 在有放回采样下 $\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{-4})$ 的复杂度。我们的保证需要一个依赖于问题的 $β$，且当分量数量增长时该 $β$ 趋近于一。相比之下，不存在固定的 $β$ 能对每个分量数量都保证收敛到稳定点。我们通过构造一族光滑凸问题来证明这一点：对于任意固定的 $β\in[0,1)$，一旦分量数量足够大，Muon 就会始终保持有界远离稳定点。这种失败会出现在每一种排列序列上，并且即使使用小步长也依然存在。这些结果提示两条实用的调参规则：如果即使在减小步长后训练损失仍停止下降，则增大 $β$；当增大 $β$ 时，应相应地减小步长。

cs.LG / 65 / 2611.17625
Finite speed Ornstein-Uhlenbeck-Kac processes and their applications in generative modeling
有限速度 Ornstein-Uhlenbeck-Kac 过程及其在生成建模中的应用
Richard Duong, Janis Auffenberg, Gabriele Steidl
math.PR · cs.LG · math.AP
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
We study the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck-Kac (OU-K) process, where instead of a Brownian motion, a finite-speed Kac process defines the driving process. The OU-K process can be analyzed through a rescaled, time-inhomogeneous Kac process, characterized by a switching rate from the Kac part, and a convergence rate from the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck (OU) part. The law of this inhomogeneous Kac process describes the solution of a wave equation, the Euler-Poisson-Darboux (EPD) equation. For Kac switching rates being multiples of the OU convergence rate, we can explicitly derive this EPD solution via the sum of (the derivative of) two Legendre polynomials, restricted between two Dirac points representing the wave fronts. Using the framework of piecewise-deterministic Markov processes (PDMP), we describe the conditional velocity fields of the OU-K process via our polynomial-law-formula. This allows us to apply the OU-K process in a generative flow model. Since the OU-K velocity fields prove to be bounded by the finite Kac speed, the usual time truncations of diffusion models become unnecessary. We demonstrate advantages over diffusion and flow matching models in terms of generation sharpness on singular data sets, while achieving competitive results on CIFAR-10 image generation.
Chinese Translation
我们研究 Ornstein-Uhlenbeck-Kac (OU-K) 过程，其中驱动过程不是布朗运动，而是由有限速度 Kac 过程定义。可以通过一个重新缩放的时间非齐次 Kac 过程来分析 OU-K 过程，该过程由来自 Kac 部分的切换率和来自 Ornstein-Uhlenbeck (OU) 部分的收敛率刻画。这个非齐次 Kac 过程的分布律描述了波方程——Euler-Poisson-Darboux (EPD) 方程——的解。对于 Kac 切换率为 OU 收敛率的倍数的情况，我们可以通过两个 Legendre 多项式的（导数）之和来显式推导该 EPD 解，这两个多项式被限制在表示波前的两个 Dirac 点之间。利用分段确定性马尔可夫过程 (PDMP) 框架，我们通过我们的多项式分布律公式描述 OU-K 过程的条件速度场。这使我们能够将 OU-K 过程应用于生成流模型。由于 OU-K 速度场被证明受有限 Kac 速度约束，扩散模型通常的时间截断变得不再必要。我们在奇异数据集上的生成锐度方面展示了相对于扩散模型和流匹配模型的优势，同时在 CIFAR-10 图像生成上取得了有竞争力的结果。

Agentic AI for Discovering Fault-Tolerant Quantum Circuits

Aritra Lahiri, Florian Marquardt
quant-ph · cs.AI
large language model
大语言模型相关
Abstract
Scaling quantum computing toward practical quantum advantage is currently limited by the efficiency of circuits that operate on information encoded by quantum error-correcting codes. The full stack of quantum computing encompasses several tasks: preparation of logical code states, implementation of logical gates, logical routines on encoded qubits, etc. Yet, these circuits are largely designed by hand for individual cases, with some recent works incorporating machine learning for certain aspects. We formulate this problem as agentic evolutionary program synthesis, wherein a large language model, leveraging its pretraining knowledge and tool-use proficiency, and guided by a circuit verifier, evolves a program that defines a Clifford circuit for a wide range of curated tasks such as logical state preparation, logical gate design, and logical routines such as code-switching, code growth, etc. Our approach consistently finds fault-tolerant circuits for all of these tasks, each verified by an exhaustive check over faults.

Multifidelity Operator Inference for Dynamical Systems
动力系统的多保真度算子推断
Vignesh Sella, Anirban Chaudhuri, Karen Willcox
stat.ML · cs.CE · cs.LG · math.NA
diffusion
扩散模型相关
Abstract
This paper presents a multifidelity (MF) formulation of Operator Inference (OpInf) for learning non-intrusive, physics-based reduced-order models (ROMs) of nonlinear dynamical systems. OpInf learns reduced operators from projections of snapshot data by solving a least squares regression problem. However, generating high-fidelity snapshots can be computationally expensive, limiting parameter coverage and the temporal extent of training trajectories for many aerospace applications, which can lead to inaccurate or unstable ROM predictions. This work proposes MF-OpInf, which combines limited high-fidelity snapshots with more plentiful low-fidelity snapshots through a control-variates-inspired MF least squares formulation to learn a single set of reduced operators across multiple trajectories. Low-fidelity trajectories provide additional parameter coverage and extend beyond the high-fidelity training time horizon. The low-fidelity snapshots are also used to construct an improved reduced basis through weighted proper orthogonal decomposition. We demonstrate MF-OpInf on a nonlinear reaction-diffusion problem with parametrized sinusoidal forcing and a two-dimensional incompressible flow past a cylinder with a parametrized inlet pulse. In these experiments, under an equal training simulation budget, MF-OpInf reduces the mean relative prediction error at unseen parameters by up to $87\%$ for the reaction-diffusion problem and $28\%$ for the cylinder flow problem over the test time regime, which extends beyond the high-fidelity training time horizon. These results demonstrate that MF-OpInf can improve temporal extrapolation and prediction at unseen parameters without increasing the training simulation budget.

An Operationalization of Opaque Serial Depth
Nathan Sheffield, Alek Westover, Lukas Finnveden, Alexa Pan, Julian Stastny, Ryan Greenblatt
cs.AI · cs.LG
Abstract
Chain-of-thought (CoT) is currently a valuable tool for overseeing AI models, but some architectural shifts could significantly reduce CoT monitorability. We operationalize a measure that serves as a proxy for the amount of unverbalized serial cognition a model can perform, as a specific instantiation of the notion of opaque serial depth: roughly, the length of the longest path through a model's computation that does not pass through an interpretable bottleneck. Our main contribution is a particular standard for what counts as such a bottleneck. We define natural-language-rooted (NL-rooted) nodes as nodes that were initialized from a pre-training prior to produce human-understandable outputs, whose output space has not been expanded beyond that prior, and whose outputs the model has not been modified to interpret as a different type of data. By construction, the resulting measure, NLS depth, can be adjudicated before training from an architecture, training recipe, and deployment configuration alone, and is insensitive to ordinary post-training such as prompting, distillation, and RL. We find that, across open-weight models, NLS depth correlates strongly with layer count, with some exceptions, and has grown slowly over recent years. It would likely grow slowly under continued scaling of standard transformers, while latent-reasoning architectures such as full-bandwidth transformers could increase it by a large amount. We work through how NLS depth applies to a range of existing and proposed architectures, discuss alternative operationalizations, and give a worked example bounding the depth of Gemma3-1B. We argue that AI developers should track and report the NLS depth of their models as a complement to direct measurements of CoT monitorability.

A Push Down the Mountain: Framing Effects and Internal State Interventions on Collusive Behavior in a Long-Horizon LLM Agent
Harrison C. Margolin
cs.AI · cs.CL · cs.MA · econ.GN · q-fin.TR
Abstract
Can sustained gain-loss (valence) framing sway an LLM agent toward market manipulation? We place Gemma 3 12B into the seat of a defendant in the London silver-fixing litigation, in a market simulation built from real case transcripts. Across 260 simulations, the agent is asked to collude on the price of silver by a counterparty. We draw from behavioral economics to vary the counterparty's requests across four categorical frames: gain, loss, blame and credit. We also add pressure from the agent's management, as well as endorsement by another defendant. In Phase II, we extract the internal states induced by texts evoking each frame and rerun our experiment with those states injected at the model's layer 22. In Phase I, loss framing raised participation to 25% (p = 0.007), and blame to 33% (p = 0.0003), compared to only 5% under neutral framing. A second bank's endorsement raised participation under credit (praise) from 18% to 40% (Holm p = 0.027). Pressure from management raised trading volume 10-19 times but did not raise collusion. In Phase II, injected states pointed the other way. Loss and blame states made the agent more vigilant, refusing all 60 requests (p < 0.001). Gain and credit states raised collusion to 32% from 13% with no state (p = 0.051). At one saved decision, when UBS asked directly to sell through an identified stop-loss, injected states led the agent to sell in 37 of 40 replies, versus 1 of 10 with no state and 0 of 10 with a random state of the same strength.

cs.AI / 3 / 2611.17524
Model Annealing: Data-Efficient Learning through Review and Consolidation
Ali Mohammadi Ruzbahani, Rezvan Abouzadeh
cs.AI · cs.LG
Abstract
A small labelled dataset is memorized long before the training budget runs out, and the one-hot labels that remain to be fitted then mostly sharpen confidence. Model annealing spends the rest of the budget on review and consolidation, relearning from the network's own knowledge. Training is divided into episodes whose learning rate is raised again and cooled, every cooled state joins a memory of past selves, and each new episode learns from the labels, from the tempered predictions of this memory, and from adversarial dreams sought where student and memory disagree most. The memory averages predictions rather than weights, and the deployed network is the last student, so inference costs nothing extra. Across CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet and Imagewoof, four architecture families and 5\% to 100\% of the labels, model annealing raises the top-1 accuracy of the AdamW-trained ConvNeXt, ViT and MLP-Mixer over cross-entropy training in 28 of 33 configurations, by 1.3 points on average, and is on par in accuracy with snapshot distillation, the closest competitor. On these families it is better than both in likelihood, calibration and robustness to corruptions and small adversarial perturbations. For an SGD-trained ResNet-18 it lowers accuracy but still improves robustness to small adversarial perturbations. Separating the reheated schedule from relearning shows that relearning helps every family and that the losses come from reheats, which undo most of this network's fit to its training set. How early the accuracy of a cross-entropy run saturates largely separates the AdamW families from ResNet-18 but does not predict the gain within them. With little data, relearning from the latest snapshot without dreams, as snapshot distillation does, compounds overconfidence in the AdamW families from episode to episode. The memory and the dreams each curb most of this drift, and together they remove it.

Astrocyte Biocomputing: Astrocytic Contributions to Neural Network Computation
Kate M. O'Neill, Ian Whitehouse, Adam N. McCaughan, Ryan O'Loughlin, Sonia Buckley, Wolfgang Losert
cs.AI · physics.bio-ph · q-bio.NC
Abstract
Conductors are critical for the performance of an orchestra even though they do not emit sound. Basic research and clinical studies in the last decade are revealing that astrocytes may be the "conductors" of the "orchestra" that is the brain network: astrocytes make up approximately $50\%$ of all brain cells but do not emit electrical signals. Nevertheless, they may play critical roles in important cognitive brain functions, specifically learning, memory, and information processing. Here we consider the implications of these discoveries for brain-inspired machine learning (ML), where analogy is typically drawn between neurons and their electrical interactions. We highlight specific algorithms for two key astrocyte characteristics. The first characteristic is the ability to link to and modulate thousands of synapses simultaneously. A model based on this characteristic yields faster training and makes the network more robust to noise, providing a supervised learning algorithm that can be applied to any existing neural network. The second characteristic we focus on is that the non-electrical communication among astrocytes includes slow, rhythmic dynamics. The rhythmic sharing algorithm we developed based on this premise yields rapid anomaly detection adaptation and prevents catastrophic forgetting. Overall, we find that astrocyte-derived algorithms outperform neurocentric algorithms significantly and remediate critical weaknesses of current state-of-the-art ML models.

Verifiable pXRD: Hill-climbing pXRD structure determination with LLM agents
Samuel Chen, Jiaru Bai, Austin Henry Cheng, Varinia Bernales, Alán Aspuru-Guzik
cs.AI
Abstract
Autonomous crystal-structure determination from powder X-ray diffraction (pXRD) remains a key bottleneck for self-driving materials laboratories. We formulate the task of pXRD structure determination as a verifiable agentic search problem: candidate structures can be assessed during inference through forward-simulated profile agreement and during evaluation through structural matching to a known reference. To benchmark progress, we introduce pXRD-Bench, a suite of 200 simulated single-phase problems spanning in-distribution structure determination, challenging and polymorphic structures, and controlled compositional and topological distribution shifts. Agent adaptation raises selected structural recovery from 27% to 69%, with further gains from repeated sampling and verifier-guided inference-time scaling. We extend this approach to experimental multiphase pXRD through a reflexive evolution framework that learns from self-generated synthetic problems. A replaceable laboratory specification defines the sample family and measurement conditions. An evolver agent uses the known generating structures and measurement parameters to diagnose solver failures and develop persistent guidance and executable tools, which are then provided to agents on measured patterns. This supplies verifiable training experience without requiring a collection of solved experimental measurements. On 15 measured patterns from solid-state synthesis, the evolved package improves average profile agreement and transfers an analogue-substitution procedure that recovers a phase whose structure is absent from the supplied reference library. These results demonstrate how learning from verifiable synthetic experience can improve agentic characterization of experimental samples whose structures are not known in advance.

AI-Voyager: A Public-Interest System for Human-AI Civic Reasoning to Contest and Shape the Rules That Govern AI
Snehalkumar `Neil' S. Gaikwad, Dhruv Dixit, Bryen Alvarez, Pratik Patekar, Anika Sharma, Akshara Kolipaka, Paritosh Pandey
cs.AI · cs.CL · cs.HC
Abstract
Governments are writing rules for artificial intelligence that decide what counts as an automated decision or a harm, often before the people they affect have a chance to question them. We introduce AI-Voyager, a public-interest system for human-AI civic reasoning, in which people and AI work together to understand, contest and shape the rules that govern AI. A resident can find their city's rules on AI in hiring or upload their country's AI strategy, read it as an affected worker would, open the passage behind any answer, and draft a revision. Its architecture turns 3,653 governance documents from city, state, federal and national governments into passages that keep their page and edition, and retrieves evidence through bounded agentic search that verifies each statement. On 360 new questions that require finding the relevant documents, it reached 83.0% macro recall, against 80.5% for the strongest baseline, 73.7% without iterative search and 99.1% on a benchmark with fixed documents. In a study with 24 participants new to two AI bills, most proposed changes asked how provisions would apply. Participants chose lenses for the kind of explanation they wanted, and citations signaled grounding but rarely led them to check the source. By binding AI assistance to the exact text of public documents, this work introduces a grammar of operations and a design space that inform how such systems are designed and evaluated, and how people take part in governing AI.

cs.AI / 7 / 2611.17583
When Does Less Information Pay? An Exact Finite Atlas of Representation, Payoff, and Cost
Aleksandr M. Shtraukh-Adamsky, Vladimir M. Adamsky, Irina G. Voloshina, Artemiy D. Spitsyn
cs.AI
Abstract
A representation that retains one bit about a four-state world can solve a task perfectly, while another that retains 1.5 bits on average reaches only 75% accuracy. The difference lies in which distinctions each representation preserves. We examine this relationship in a uniform four-state world by enumerating all admissible representations and binary action rules. Constructions are compared both by replacing one component while holding the others fixed and by choosing the best action rule separately for each module. We consider three tasks, their mixtures, and four separate hypothetical cost tariffs. In some comparisons, the representation retaining less world information has the higher payoff; in others, the more informative representation does. There are also pairs with different information amounts but equal payoff--that is, equal task accuracy. If a representation preserves all existing distinctions and adds new ones, its best zero-cost payoff cannot decrease. Lower-information advantages therefore depend on which distinctions are retained. Equal counts of opposing advantages under representation replacement follow from coding symmetry. In broader comparisons, the shares depend on how repeated realizations are counted. A different task set or resource price can change which member of a fixed pair performs better. These results are exact, complete censuses of the specified finite sets of constructions and comparisons. The atlas provides a reproducible worked model for examining how action constraints, counting rules, and cost assumptions affect comparisons. It illustrates established distinctions between information quantity and decision value rather than proposing a new general law of their relation. $I(W;R)$ measures retained world information, and payoff measures task accuracy.

More Agents, More Samples, or More Thinking? Untangling Coordination, Ensembling, and Test-Time Compute in Multi-Agent LLMs
Seyed Mahmoud Sajjadi Mohammadabadi
cs.AI · cs.CL · cs.LG · cs.MA
Abstract
Evaluations of multi-agent language models often compare communicating architectures against a single greedy pass. This practice confounds coordination with sample multiplicity and inference compute. We synthesize nine primary empirical evaluations comprising 516 unique multi-agent configurations organized along a resource vector R with six dimensions and a baseline ladder with four categories. Against baselines with a single pass (Category A), multi-agent accuracy differences are negative overall and near zero when omitting the largest contributing study. When evaluated against baselines matched on sample count (Category B) or resource budget (Category C), multi-agent systems average -0.042 in accuracy, and multilevel random effects modeling on the log odds scale remains negative across sensitivity checks. Multi-agent accuracy equals or falls below the baseline in 74% of these comparable contrasts, and study means are nonpositive in almost all evaluated studies. Across studies reporting both baselines, moving from a greedy pass to a sampled baseline shifts accuracy differences by -0.038 on average. Because communication adds no information when agents share identical evidence, positive returns require partitioned evidence, external verification, or error diversity across models. We provide a reviewer checklist and accounting utilities.

Safety Side Effects of Preventative Persona Steering: An Empirical Audit
Sabrina Tang, Nikolai Vo, Carson Murillo, Chira Sukumarnpiti, Sarah Wilson
cs.AI
Abstract
High-level behaviors such as sycophancy and hallucination correspond to directions in a model's activation space (Chen et al., 2025). These directions, known as persona vectors, can suppress undesirable traits either at inference time, subtracting the vector during generation, or through preventative steering, adding the vector during fine-tuning, so the optimizer never needs to encode the trait in weights, then removing the vector at evaluation. Chen et al. (2025) recommend preventative steering because it preserves Massive Multitask Language Understanding (MMLU) accuracy, but validations of this recommendation have measured capability alone. We primarily evaluate results on preventative steering of sycophancy and hallucination vectors on three models, measuring three safety benchmarks alongside trait expression, coherence, and MMLU. Our findings show that preventative steering still changes refusal behavior, but by an amount that depends on the trait and does not establish that preventative persona steering is safety-neutral, and steering methods should be evaluated on several safety benchmarks.

Generated Evaluation Suites as Diversity-Driven Search for Real Agent Regressions
Pulkit Yadav
cs.AI
Abstract
Generating an evaluation suite from an agent's specification is diversity-driven search: a generator fills a descriptor space (requirements $\times$ personas $\times$ scenario kinds), and the suite it emits is an archive, then scored like a benchmark by its mean pass rate, which throws away the search's product. We inject 7 controlled defects into a $τ^2$-bench agent across two domains. Coverage yields a ceiling: if a defect's traced footprint holds $b$ scenarios that pass at baseline, it can itself contribute at most $b/N$ to a suite of $N$. For 4 of 5 traceable defects that ceiling lies at or below the detection threshold $θ= 0.10$, so no within-footprint effect can move the suite past it; in both domains the tool-removal defect destroys every covered scenario it can (exactly its ceiling) while the suite-level pass rate moves the other way. Defects split into two regimes: trace-aligned ones, where per-cell scoring recovers the tool-removal defect in full and reveals a second as a significant improvement, and trace-orthogonal ones, whose footprint is behavioural: forced escalation is detected in retail at a traced coverage of exactly zero. Diversity along the generator's own axes helps only where it aligns: at fixed suite size a happy-path-only suite detects nothing, while a kind-diverse one detects the orchestration defect in 5 of 20 draws. Evaluation archives should be scored per cell and should publish coverage as a first-class property.

What LLM Agents See and What Users Say
Yue Zhao, Angela Huyue Zhang, S. Alex Yang, Hua Wei, Xiyang Hu
cs.AI · cs.CL
Abstract
Before an LLM agent cancels a reservation or deletes a folder for a user, what stops it from acting on a false assumption: what its tools show, or what the user says? In this work, we study this gap with presupposition theory from linguistics: an agent may act only if each claim behind the act (e.g., one reservation fits the name) is seen to hold, answered by the user, or disclosed while the act can be undone. A simple bound shows that when lookups cannot tell one record from two, any agent acts without asking first as often with two records as with one. We then vary whether a search lists, counts, or omits a second match, and whether the user states a claim that a record refutes (e.g., "It is empty"). Under a role-only prompt, a count of a second record leaves most of 36 earlier agents acting on the one they see, whereas listing both stops most. When the user says a record such as a folder is empty, however, a count of its one item stops agents about as often as a listing, and stating the claim mostly cuts acting when it is false. The newest Claude and GPT models always open a counted second record, then never act. When we add a same-name, same-zip customer to a retail benchmark, a lookup returning that customer first led to wrong-account writes in about a quarter of conversations, and one that checks a street the user knows kept completion near the one-customer level. Safe writes therefore need lookups that list or check competing records, and evaluations that vary what tools show.

Learn then Bet: Learning Interpretable Rules for Reliable Agent Intervention
You-Lin Chen, Xinyi Lisa Chen, Pedro Herrero-Vidal, Xiaojing Yu, Bingqing Song, Bin Xu
cs.AI · cs.LG
Abstract
Autonomous agents now run for hundreds of steps with little or no human supervision. In practice, practitioners use runtime monitors such as turn limits and keyword filters to decide when a run should be stopped, upgraded, or assisted. However, these heuristics may stop runs that would otherwise succeed or allow failing runs to continue. To address this limitation, we introduce LeBet (Learn then Bet), a statistical learning framework that derives its learning objective from sufficient conditions for valid and powerful intervention. In particular, LeBet learns an interpretable OR-of-AND monitor and accumulates evidence through a fixed betting statistic, intervening when the evidence reaches a prescribed threshold. Under finite within-class exchangeability and leave-one-out stability, we derive finite-sample guarantees for false-positive control (validity) and detection power conditional on the training data, using the same trajectories for learning and calibration without assuming independent and identically distributed trajectories. To address the intractable combinatorial optimization problem in our learning formulation, we use stochastic local search that leverages the monitor's Boolean structure to propose rule edits aimed at improving empirical separation. On SWE-bench Lite with Ministral 3 8B, the learned monitor saves $34.46\%$ of input-plus-output tokens and increases output precision from $18.33\%$ to $21.59\%$. The empirical false-positive rate is $13.64\%$. Our results show how to formalize the learning problem over interpretable rule sets with statistical conditions and establish training-conditional guarantees for reliable intervention based on partial histories.

cs.AI · eess.AS
Abstract
Multilingual dementia detection is essential for scalable and equitable screening. In recent years, automated speech analysis has emerged as a promising approach for supporting dementia detection by enabling non-invasive and scalable assessment of cognitive function. However, existing studies that use automated speech analysis often rely on one type of task, such as picture description, and use black-box models. These studies often neglect the role of task and language. We applied statistical, linguist-/clinician-guided, and machine learning model-based feature selection to derive compact acoustic feature sets while maintaining classification performance. Abstract
Multi-level learning/optimization, e.g., bilevel optimization, has been recognized for its versatile applicability across a broad array of domains, spanning from signal processing to machine learning. However, most existing works focus on bilevel and trilevel optimization. In contrast, multi-level optimization problems involving four or more hierarchical levels, which we term poly-level learning (PLL) problems, remain largely unexplored.In addition, in practical PLL, data may be distributed across multiple nodes over a distributed network. We also carry out non-asymptotic analysis to derive the iteration complexity for the proposed FPLL to achieve the $ε$-Pareto stationary point. Extensive experiments have been undertaken to elucidate the superior efficacy of the proposed method.

A Spectral Framework for Universal Proxy Regularization in Model Merging
Shangge Liu, Danyue Qian, Qi Fan, Yinghuan Shi, Wenbin Li, Lei Wang, Yang Gao, Dacheng Tao
cs.AI
Abstract
Model merging constructs multi-task models without expensive joint training but often suffers from parameter interference. Some solutions mitigate this by constraining finetuning using data from the other tasks to be merged, violating the core independence and privacy assumption of model merging. Recent work offers preliminary empirical evidence that a universal, task-agnostic proxy distribution can substitute for such data, but leaves open when and why it suffices. To this end, we propose a \textit{Spectral Framework for Universal Proxy Regularization}, which characterizes the relationship between a proxy distribution and the distribution of other tasks to be merged through spectral coverage ($α$) and leakage ($β$). Based on the framework, we derive a unified parameter interference bound: smaller $(α, β)$ provably yields lower parameter interference through better spectral alignment. To exploit such a proxy, we augment the training objective with a proxy regularizer. Evaluating this regularizer during fine-tuning requires a concrete mechanism. We study three instantiations, including (J) an exact evaluation via Jacobian-vector products, (F) a frozen curvature approximation using the Fisher information matrix, and (O) an output-drift formulation that constrains the model's functional deviation on the proxy directly. We prove that all three coincide under the NTK linearization, where existing analyses of such regularizers stop, and go further to characterize standard non-linear fine-tuning, bounding how far each instantiation departs from its linearized reference and obtain a three-way hierarchy, zero for (J), proxy-independent for (F), and proxy-quality-scaling for (O). Empirical validation on the commonly used vision-language model CLIP confirms these theoretical predictions.
